Type Variance in Scala\n\n**Covariance (+T)**: Type parameters that only appear in output positions\n- Used for containers that produce values\n- Example: `List[+A]` - can safely use `List[Dog]` where `List[Animal]` is expected\n```scala\n// Covariant: output position only\nclass Container[+T](val items: Seq[T]) {\n  def getAll: Seq[T] = items\n}\n```\n\n**Contravariance (-T)**: Type parameters that only appear in input positions\n- Used for functions/sinks that consume values\n- Example: `Function1[-A, +B]` - can safely use `(Animal => String)` where `(Dog => String)` is expected\n```scala\n// Contravariant: input position only\ntrait Consumer[-T] {\n  def consume(item: T): Unit\n}\n```\n\n**Invariance (T)**: Default - requires exact type match\n- Used when type can be both read and written\n```scala\n// Invariant: read and write\nclass Handler[T](var value: T) {\n  def get: T = value\n  def set(newValue: T): Unit = { value = newValue }\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Type Bounds\n\n**Upper Bounds** (`T <: U`): Type parameter must be subtype of U\n```scala\nclass NumericTokenizer[T <: Numeric[T]](implicit num: Numeric[T]) {\n  def tokenize(value: T): String = value.toString\n}\n```\n\n**Lower Bounds** (`T >: U`): Type parameter must be supertype of U\n```scala\ndef process[T >: String](value: T): T = value\n```\n\n**Context Bounds** (`T : Ord`): Implicit evidence that Ord[T] exists\n```scala\ndef sort[T : Ordering](items: Seq[T]): Seq[T] = items.sorted\n```\n\n### 3. Type Classes for Duck Typing\n\nScala's type classes replace Python's Protocol duck typing:\n\n```scala\n// Type class definition\ntrait Tokenizable[T] {\n  def toToken(value: T): String\n}\n\n// Implicit evidence\nobject Tokenizable {\n  implicit val stringTokenizable: Tokenizable[String] = (s: String) => s\n  implicit val intTokenizable: Tokenizable[Int] = (i: Int) => i.toString\n}\n\n// Usage with context bound\ndef tokenize[T : Tokenizable](value: T): String = {\n  implicitly[Tokenizable[T]].toToken(value)\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Sealed Traits for Discriminated Unions\n\nReplaces Python's Union types with exhaustive pattern matching:\n\n```scala\nsealed trait JsonValue\ncase object JsonNull extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonString(value: String) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonNumber(value: Double) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonArray(values: Seq[JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonObject(values: Map[String, JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\n```\n\n## Key Differences from Python\n\n| Python | Scala |\n|--------|-------|\n| `Protocol` (structural typing) | Type classes (nominal typing with implicits) |\n| `Union[A, B]` | Sealed trait hierarchy |\n| `Optional[T]` | `Option[T]` (more idiomatic) |\n| `Callable[[A], B]` | `A => B` (function type) |\n| Runtime type checks | Compile-time type checking with pattern matching |\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use type classes over protocol-like traits** - More idiomatic and composable\n2. **Leverage variance annotations** - Makes APIs more flexible and type-safe\n3. **Use sealed traits** - Enables exhaustive pattern matching\n4. **Pattern matching over instanceof** - More functional, less error-prone\n5. **Implicit evidence over runtime checks** - Compile-time safety\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":136,"cache_read_input_tokens":25632,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":136,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0a1caff8-68b0-47f1-8d8b-3a7ea67e28c4","uuid":"6e3331b5-b682-42a1-934f-0ed1a7c22a73"}

Type Variance in Scala\n\n**Covariance (+T)**: Type parameters that only appear in output positions\n- Used for containers that produce values\n- Example: `List[+A]` - can safely use `List[Dog]` where `List[Animal]` is expected\n```scala\n// Covariant: output position only\nclass Container[+T](val items: Seq[T]) {\n  def getAll: Seq[T] = items\n}\n```\n\n**Contravariance (-T)**: Type parameters that only appear in input positions\n- Used for functions/sinks that consume values\n- Example: `Function1[-A, +B]` - can safely use `(Animal => String)` where `(Dog => String)` is expected\n```scala\n// Contravariant: input position only\ntrait Consumer[-T] {\n  def consume(item: T): Unit\n}\n```\n\n**Invariance (T)**: Default - requires exact type match\n- Used when type can be both read and written\n```scala\n// Invariant: read and write\nclass Handler[T](var value: T) {\n  def get: T = value\n  def set(newValue: T): Unit = { value = newValue }\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Type Bounds\n\n**Upper Bounds** (`T <: U`): Type parameter must be subtype of U\n```scala\nclass NumericTokenizer[T <: Numeric[T]](implicit num: Numeric[T]) {\n  def tokenize(value: T): String = value.toString\n}\n```\n\n**Lower Bounds** (`T >: U`): Type parameter must be supertype of U\n```scala\ndef process[T >: String](value: T): T = value\n```\n\n**Context Bounds** (`T : Ord`): Implicit evidence that Ord[T] exists\n```scala\ndef sort[T : Ordering](items: Seq[T]): Seq[T] = items.sorted\n```\n\n### 3. Type Classes for Duck Typing\n\nScala's type classes replace Python's Protocol duck typing:\n\n```scala\n// Type class definition\ntrait Tokenizable[T] {\n  def toToken(value: T): String\n}\n\n// Implicit evidence\nobject Tokenizable {\n  implicit val stringTokenizable: Tokenizable[String] = (s: String) => s\n  implicit val intTokenizable: Tokenizable[Int] = (i: Int) => i.toString\n}\n\n// Usage with context bound\ndef tokenize[T : Tokenizable](value: T): String = {\n  implicitly[Tokenizable[T]].toToken(value)\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Sealed Traits for Discriminated Unions\n\nReplaces Python's Union types with exhaustive pattern matching:\n\n```scala\nsealed trait JsonValue\ncase object JsonNull extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonString(value: String) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonNumber(value: Double) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonArray(values: Seq[JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonObject(values: Map[String, JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\n```\n\n## Key Differences from Python\n\n| Python | Scala |\n|--------|-------|\n| `Protocol` (structural typing) | Type classes (nominal typing with implicits) |\n| `Union[A, B]` | Sealed trait hierarchy |\n| `Optional[T]` | `Option[T]` (more idiomatic) |\n| `Callable[[A], B]` | `A => B` (function type) |\n| Runtime type checks | Compile-time type checking with pattern matching |\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use type classes over protocol-like traits** - More idiomatic and composable\n2. **Leverage variance annotations** - Makes APIs more flexible and type-safe\n3. **Use sealed traits** - Enables exhaustive pattern matching\n4. **Pattern matching over instanceof** - More functional, less error-prone\n5. **Implicit evidence over runtime checks** - Compile-time safety\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Functor and Monad Patterns\n\n### Functor (map operation)\n```scala\ntrait Functor[F[_]] {\n  def map[A, B](fa: F[A])(f: A => B): F[B]\n}\n\n// Example: Option as Functor\nimplicit val optionFunctor: Functor[Option] = new Functor[Option] {\n  def map[A, B](opt: Option[A])(f: A => B): Option[B] = opt.map(f)\n}\n```\n\n### Monad (flatMap + pure operations)\n```scala\ntrait Monad[F[_]] extends Functor[F] {\n  def pure[A](a: A): F[A]\n  def flatMap[A, B](fa: F[A])(f: A => F[B]): F[B]\n\n  // Derived map implementation\n  def map[A, B](fa: F[A])(f: A => B): F[B] =\n    flatMap(fa)(a => pure(f(a)))\n}\n```\n\n### For-comprehensions (syntactic sugar for monads)\n```scala\n// For-comprehension automatically translates to flatMap chains\nval result = for {\n  a <- Option(1)\n  b <- Option(2)\n  c <- Option(3)\n} yield a + b + c\n\n// Equivalent to:\n// Option(1).flatMap(a => Option(2).flatMap(b => Option(3).map(c => a + b + c)))\n```\n\n## 2. Scala Collections API (Immutability First)\n\n### Preferred Collections\n```scala\n// Immutable is default in scala.collection.immutable\nval list: List[Int] = List(1, 2, 3)\nval set: Set[String] = Set(\"a\", \"b\", \"c\")\nval map: Map[String, Int] = Map(\"x\" -> 1, \"y\" -> 2)\nval seq: Seq[Double] = Seq(1.0, 2.0, 3.0)\nval vector: Vector[String] = Vector(\"a\", \"b\", \"c\")\n\n// Mutable alternatives (use sparingly)\nval mutableList = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[Int]()\nval mutableMap = scala.collection.mutable.Map[String, String]()\n```\n\n### Collections Operations\n```scala\n// Lazy evaluation with Iterator\ndef tokenizeIterator[T](values: Iterator[T]): Iterator[String] = {\n  values.map(_.toString)\n}\n\n// Map, filter, fold operations\nList(1, 2, 3, 4)\n  .filter(_ > 2)\n  .map(_ * 2)\n  .fold(0)(_ + _)  // Result: 14\n\n// GroupBy for categorization\nSeq(\"apple\", \"apricot\", \"banana\", \"blueberry\")\n  .groupBy(_.head)  // Group by first letter\n```\n\n### Lazy Evaluation\n```scala\n// Lazy views for large collections\nval lazyTransform = List(1, 2, 3, 4, 5)\n  .view\n  .map(_ * 2)\n  .filter(_ > 5)\n  .force  // Convert back to strict List\n\n// Streams for potentially infinite sequences (deprecated in newer Scala)\n// Use LazyList instead\ndef naturalNumbers: LazyList[Int] = 1 #:: naturalNumbers.map(_ + 1)\n```\n\n## 3. Either for Error Handling\n\nInstead of exceptions or Optional, use `Either[Error, Result]`:\n\n```scala\nsealed trait TokenError\ncase class InvalidFormat(msg: String) extends TokenError\ncase class EncodingError(msg: String) extends TokenError\n\ndef tokenize(value: String): Either[TokenError, String] = {\n  if (value.isEmpty) Left(InvalidFormat(\"Empty value\"))\n  else Right(value.toUpperCase)\n}\n\n// Using Either with for-comprehension\ndef processMultiple(values: Seq[String]): Either[TokenError, Seq[String]] = {\n  val results = values.map(tokenize(_))\n  // Collect first error or all results\n  results.foldRight[Either[TokenError, Seq[String]]](Right(Seq())) {\n    case (Left(err), _) => Left(err)\n    case (Right(v), Right(vs)) => Right(v +: vs)\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## 4. Iterator vs Iterable vs Seq\n\n```scala\n// Iterable[T]: Can be iterated multiple times\nval iterable: Iterable[Int] = List(1, 2, 3)\niterable.foreach(println)  // OK\niterable.foreach(println)  // OK again\n\n// Iterator[T]: Single-pass, lazy evaluation\nval iterator: Iterator[Int] = iterable.iterator\niterator.foreach(println)   // OK\niterator.foreach(println)   // Does nothing - already consumed\n\n// Seq[T]: Indexed access with stable size\nval seq: Seq[Int] = List(1, 2, 3)\nprintln(seq(0))  // Access by index\n```\n\n## 5. They provide automatic implementations of useful methods and are typically immutable by default.\n\n## 1. Case Class Basics\n\n### Definition and Auto-Generated Methods\n```scala\ncase class Token(\n  value: String,\n  tokenType: String,\n  metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map()\n)\n\n// Automatically provides:\n// - constructor with default parameter values\n// - apply factory method (no 'new' needed)\n// - unapply for pattern matching\n// - equals() and hashCode()\n// - toString()\n// - copy() for immutable updates\n\n// Usage\nval token = Token(\"hello\", \"STRING\")  // No 'new' needed\nval token2 = Token(\"world\", \"STRING\", Map(\"lang\" -> \"en\"))\n```\n\n### Immutable Updates with copy()\n```scala\nval original = Token(\"hello\", \"STRING\")\n\n// Add metadata using copy\nval updated = original.copy(metadata = original.metadata + (\"key\" -> \"value\"))\n\n// Only specified fields change; others remain the same\nval changed = original.copy(value = \"goodbye\")\n// Result: Token(\"goodbye\", \"STRING\", Map())\n\n// Original is never modified\nassert(original.value == \"hello\")\n```\n\n### Pattern Matching with Case Classes\n```scala\ndef processToken(token: Token): String = token match {\n  case Token(value, \"STRING\", meta) if meta.nonEmpty =>\n    s\"String with metadata: $value\"\n  case Token(value, \"NUMERIC\", _) =>\n    s\"Number: $value\"\n  case Token(\"NULL\", _, _) =>\n    \"Null token\"\n  case Token(v, t, _) =>\n    s\"Token($v, $t)\"\n}\n\n// Destructuring in for-loops\nval tokens = List(\n  Token(\"hello\", \"STRING\"),\n  Token(\"42\", \"NUMERIC\")\n)\n\nfor {\n  Token(v, t, _) <- tokens\n  if t == \"STRING\"\n} println(s\"String: $v\")\n```\n\n## 2. Making Case Classes Truly Immutable\n\n### Sealed Hierarchy for Inheritance\n```scala\nsealed trait TokenType\ncase object STRING extends TokenType\ncase object NUMERIC extends TokenType\ncase object TEMPORAL extends TokenType\n\n// Now TokenType can only be these specific values\ndef processType(tokenType: TokenType): String = tokenType match {\n  case STRING => \"string\"\n  case NUMERIC => \"numeric\"\n  case TEMPORAL => \"temporal\"\n  // Compiler ensures exhaustive matching\n}\n```\n\n### Immutable Collections in Case Classes\n```scala\n// GOOD: Immutable collections\ncase class TokenBatch(\n  tokens: List[Token] = List(),\n  metadata: Map[String, String] = Map()\n)\n\n// Usage\nval batch = TokenBatch()\nval batch2 = batch.copy(tokens = batch.tokens :+ Token(\"new\", \"STRING\"))\n\n// BAD: Never expose mutable collections\n// case class BadBatch(tokens: scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[Token])\n```\n\n### Private Constructor Pattern\n```scala\n// When you need more control over construction\ncase class TokenRegistry private (\n  private val _registry: Map[String, List[Token]] = Map()\n) {\n  def register(key: String, tokens: List[Token]): TokenRegistry = {\n    this.copy(_registry = _registry + (key -> tokens))\n  }\n\n  def get(key: String): Option[List[Token]] = _registry.get(key)\n}\n\nobject TokenRegistry {\n  def empty: TokenRegistry = TokenRegistry()\n}\n\n// Usage: Only through the public constructor and factory methods\nval registry = TokenRegistry.empty\n```\n\n## 3. Enum Pattern for Sum Types\n\nFor Scala 3+, or simulating Enum behavior in Scala 2:\n\n```scala\nsealed trait TokenType\nobject TokenType {\n  case object STRING extends TokenType\n  case object NUMERIC extends TokenType\n  case object TEMPORAL extends TokenType\n  case object STRUCTURED extends TokenType\n\n  def fromString(s: String): Option[TokenType] = s match {\n    case \"string\" => Some(STRING)\n    case \"numeric\" => Some(NUMERIC)\n    case \"temporal\" => Some(TEMPORAL)\n    case \"structured\" => Some(STRUCTURED)\n    case _ => None\n  }\n}\n\n// Usage\nval t1: TokenType = TokenType.STRING\nval t2 = TokenType.fromString(\"numeric\")  // Option[TokenType]\n```\n\n## 4. Companion Object Pattern\n\n```scala\ncase class Token(value: String, tokenType: String, metadata: Map[String, Any])\n\nobject Token {\n  // Factory method with defaults\n  def apply(value: String, tokenType: String): Token =\n    Token(value, tokenType, Map())\n\n  // Additional constructors for common cases\n  def stringToken(value: String): Token =\n    Token(value, \"STRING\", Map())\n\n  def numericToken(value: Double): Token =\n    Token(value.toString, \"NUMERIC\", Map(\"original_type\" -> \"Double\"))\n\n  // Parsing\n  def parse(json: String): Either[String, Token] = {\n    // Implementation here\n    Left(\"Not implemented\")\n  }\n\n  // Constants\n  val NULL_TOKEN = Token(\"NULL\", \"NULL\", Map())\n}\n\n// Usage\nval t1 = Token.stringToken(\"hello\")\nval t2 = Token.NULL_TOKEN\n```\n\n## 5. Value Classes for Zero-Cost Abstraction\n\n```scala\n// Lightweight wrapper with no runtime overhead\ncase class TokenValue(value: String) extends AnyVal {\n  def length: Int = value.length\n  def toUpperCase: TokenValue = TokenValue(value.toUpperCase)\n}\n\n// At runtime, TokenValue is erased - it's just a String\n// No boxing/unboxing overhead\n```\n\n## 6. They provide automatic implementations of useful methods and are typically immutable by default.\n\n## 1. Case Class Basics\n\n### Definition and Auto-Generated Methods\n```scala\ncase class Token(\n  value: String,\n  tokenType: String,\n  metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map()\n)\n\n// Automatically provides:\n// - constructor with default parameter values\n// - apply factory method (no 'new' needed)\n// - unapply for pattern matching\n// - equals() and hashCode()\n// - toString()\n// - copy() for immutable updates\n\n// Usage\nval token = Token(\"hello\", \"STRING\")  // No 'new' needed\nval token2 = Token(\"world\", \"STRING\", Map(\"lang\" -> \"en\"))\n```\n\n### Immutable Updates with copy()\n```scala\nval original = Token(\"hello\", \"STRING\")\n\n// Add metadata using copy\nval updated = original.copy(metadata = original.metadata + (\"key\" -> \"value\"))\n\n// Only specified fields change; others remain the same\nval changed = original.copy(value = \"goodbye\")\n// Result: Token(\"goodbye\", \"STRING\", Map())\n\n// Original is never modified\nassert(original.value == \"hello\")\n```\n\n### Pattern Matching with Case Classes\n```scala\ndef processToken(token: Token): String = token match {\n  case Token(value, \"STRING\", meta) if meta.nonEmpty =>\n    s\"String with metadata: $value\"\n  case Token(value, \"NUMERIC\", _) =>\n    s\"Number: $value\"\n  case Token(\"NULL\", _, _) =>\n    \"Null token\"\n  case Token(v, t, _) =>\n    s\"Token($v, $t)\"\n}\n\n// Destructuring in for-loops\nval tokens = List(\n  Token(\"hello\", \"STRING\"),\n  Token(\"42\", \"NUMERIC\")\n)\n\nfor {\n  Token(v, t, _) <- tokens\n  if t == \"STRING\"\n} println(s\"String: $v\")\n```\n\n## 2. Making Case Classes Truly Immutable\n\n### Sealed Hierarchy for Inheritance\n```scala\nsealed trait TokenType\ncase object STRING extends TokenType\ncase object NUMERIC extends TokenType\ncase object TEMPORAL extends TokenType\n\n// Now TokenType can only be these specific values\ndef processType(tokenType: TokenType): String = tokenType match {\n  case STRING => \"string\"\n  case NUMERIC => \"numeric\"\n  case TEMPORAL => \"temporal\"\n  // Compiler ensures exhaustive matching\n}\n```\n\n### Immutable Collections in Case Classes\n```scala\n// GOOD: Immutable collections\ncase class TokenBatch(\n  tokens: List[Token] = List(),\n  metadata: Map[String, String] = Map()\n)\n\n// Usage\nval batch = TokenBatch()\nval batch2 = batch.copy(tokens = batch.tokens :+ Token(\"new\", \"STRING\"))\n\n// BAD: Never expose mutable collections\n// case class BadBatch(tokens: scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[Token])\n```\n\n### Private Constructor Pattern\n```scala\n// When you need more control over construction\ncase class TokenRegistry private (\n  private val _registry: Map[String, List[Token]] = Map()\n) {\n  def register(key: String, tokens: List[Token]): TokenRegistry = {\n    this.copy(_registry = _registry + (key -> tokens))\n  }\n\n  def get(key: String): Option[List[Token]] = _registry.get(key)\n}\n\nobject TokenRegistry {\n  def empty: TokenRegistry = TokenRegistry()\n}\n\n// Usage: Only through the public constructor and factory methods\nval registry = TokenRegistry.empty\n```\n\n## 3. Enum Pattern for Sum Types\n\nFor Scala 3+, or simulating Enum behavior in Scala 2:\n\n```scala\nsealed trait TokenType\nobject TokenType {\n  case object STRING extends TokenType\n  case object NUMERIC extends TokenType\n  case object TEMPORAL extends TokenType\n  case object STRUCTURED extends TokenType\n\n  def fromString(s: String): Option[TokenType] = s match {\n    case \"string\" => Some(STRING)\n    case \"numeric\" => Some(NUMERIC)\n    case \"temporal\" => Some(TEMPORAL)\n    case \"structured\" => Some(STRUCTURED)\n    case _ => None\n  }\n}\n\n// Usage\nval t1: TokenType = TokenType.STRING\nval t2 = TokenType.fromString(\"numeric\")  // Option[TokenType]\n```\n\n## 4. Companion Object Pattern\n\n```scala\ncase class Token(value: String, tokenType: String, metadata: Map[String, Any])\n\nobject Token {\n  // Factory method with defaults\n  def apply(value: String, tokenType: String): Token =\n    Token(value, tokenType, Map())\n\n  // Additional constructors for common cases\n  def stringToken(value: String): Token =\n    Token(value, \"STRING\", Map())\n\n  def numericToken(value: Double): Token =\n    Token(value.toString, \"NUMERIC\", Map(\"original_type\" -> \"Double\"))\n\n  // Parsing\n  def parse(json: String): Either[String, Token] = {\n    // Implementation here\n    Left(\"Not implemented\")\n  }\n\n  // Constants\n  val NULL_TOKEN = Token(\"NULL\", \"NULL\", Map())\n}\n\n// Usage\nval t1 = Token.stringToken(\"hello\")\nval t2 = Token.NULL_TOKEN\n```\n\n## 5. Value Classes for Zero-Cost Abstraction\n\n```scala\n// Lightweight wrapper with no runtime overhead\ncase class TokenValue(value: String) extends AnyVal {\n  def length: Int = value.length\n  def toUpperCase: TokenValue = TokenValue(value.toUpperCase)\n}\n\n// At runtime, TokenValue is erased - it's just a String\n// No boxing/unboxing overhead\n```\n\n## 6. Data Class Alternatives for Mutable Structures\n\n```scala\n// When you genuinely need mutability (rare!), be explicit\nclass MutableTokenBatch {\n  private var _tokens: List[Token] = List()\n\n  def tokens: List[Token] = _tokens\n  def add(token: Token): Unit = {\n    _tokens = _tokens :+ token\n  }\n\n  def clear(): Unit = {\n    _tokens = List()\n  }\n}\n\n// Or use scala.collection.mutable classes\nimport scala.collection.mutable\n\nval batch = mutable.ListBuffer[Token]()\nbatch += Token(\"hello\", \"STRING\")\nbatch += Token(\"world\", \"STRING\")\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use case classes by default** - They're the idiomatic way\n2. **Keep them immutable** - No var fields\n3. **Use sealed traits for type hierarchies** - Enables compiler checking\n4. **Use companion objects for factories** - More than just apply()\n5. **Leverage copy() for updates** - Not reassignment\n6. **Pattern match destructively** - Natural in Scala\n7. **Use Map/List for collections in case classes** - Immutable by default\n8. **Use Option[T] instead of null** - More expressive and safe\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Each has specific use cases.\n\n## 1. Option[T] - Absence Handling\n\n### When to Use\n- When a value may be absent (None) or present (Some)\n- Lightweight alternative to Optional\n- Best for operations that might not produce a result\n\n### Usage Patterns\n```scala\ndef findToken(key: String, map: Map[String, String]): Option[String] =\n  map.get(key)  // Returns Option[String]\n\n// Accessing Option values safely\nval token = findToken(\"key\", map)\n\n// Pattern matching\ntoken match {\n  case Some(value) => println(s\"Found: $value\")\n  case None => println(\"Not found\")\n}\n\n// getOrElse for defaults\nval value = token.getOrElse(\"DEFAULT\")\n\n// map and flatMap for chaining\nval uppercase = token.map(_.toUpperCase)\nval nested = token.flatMap(v => findToken(v, map))\n\n// For-comprehension\nval result = for {\n  t1 <- findToken(\"key1\", map)\n  t2 <- findToken(\"key2\", map)\n} yield t1 + t2\n```\n\n## 2. Either[L, R] - Error Context\n\n### When to Use\n- When you need to return error information (not just absence)\n- Left represents failure, Right represents success\n- Best for error handling with context\n\n### Usage Patterns\n```scala\nsealed trait TokenError\ncase class InvalidFormat(msg: String) extends TokenError\ncase class EncodingError(msg: String) extends TokenError\n\ndef tokenize(value: String): Either[TokenError, String] = {\n  if (value.isEmpty) Left(InvalidFormat(\"Empty value\"))\n  else if (value.length > 1000) Left(InvalidFormat(\"Value too long\"))\n  else Right(value.toUpperCase)\n}\n\n// Pattern matching\ntokenize(\"hello\") match {\n  case Left(InvalidFormat(msg)) => println(s\"Format error: $msg\")\n  case Left(EncodingError(msg)) => println(s\"Encoding error: $msg\")\n  case Right(token) => println(s\"Success: $token\")\n}\n\n// getOrElse\nval token = tokenize(\"hello\").getOrElse(\"DEFAULT\")\n\n// For-comprehension (fails on first Left)\nval result: Either[TokenError, String] = for {\n  t1 <- tokenize(\"value1\")\n  t2 <- tokenize(\"value2\")\n} yield t1 + t2\n\n// Handle errors explicitly\ntokenize(\"value\") match {\n  case Right(token) => println(s\"Token: $token\")\n  case Left(error) =>\n    error match {\n      case InvalidFormat(msg) => println(s\"Fix format: $msg\")\n      case EncodingError(msg) => println(s\"Fix encoding: $msg\")\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Either Extensions\n```scala\n// Converting between Option and Either\nval opt: Option[String] = Some(\"value\")\nval either = opt.toRight(InvalidFormat(\"No value\"))  // Either[InvalidFormat, String]\n\n// fold for both sides\ntokenize(\"value\").fold(\n  error => s\"Error: $error\",\n  token => s\"Success: $token\"\n)\n\n// Converting errors\ndef retry[E, A](\n  operation: () => Either[E, A],\n  maxRetries: Int = 3\n): Either[String, A] = {\n  (0 until maxRetries).foldLeft[Either[String, A]](Left(\"All retries failed\")) {\n    case (_, _) =>\n      operation() match {\n        case Right(a) => Right(a)\n        case Left(_) if maxRetries > 1 => Left(\"Retry\") // Try again\n        case Left(e) => Left(e.toString)\n      }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## 3. Try[T] - Exception Wrapper\n\n### When to Use\n- When dealing with code that throws exceptions\n- Automatically catches exceptions\n- Best for wrapping legacy/external code\n\n### Usage Patterns\n```scala\nimport scala.util.{Try, Success, Failure}\n\ndef parseToken(json: String): Try[Token] = Try {\n  // If this throws an exception, Try catches it\n  val obj = scala.util.parsing.json.JSON.parseFull(json)\n  // ... construct Token\n  Token(\"value\", \"STRING\")\n}\n\n// Pattern matching\nparseToken(jsonStr) match {\n  case Success(token) => println(s\"Parsed: $token\")\n  case Failure(ex) => println(s\"Parse error: ${ex.getMessage}\")\n}\n\n// map, flatMap, for-comprehension\nval result = for {\n  token <- parseToken(str1)\n  value <- Try(token.value.toInt)\n} yield value\n```\n\n## 4. Validation Patterns\n\n### Accumulating Errors\n```scala\nsealed trait ValidationError\ncase class MissingField(name: String) extends ValidationError\ncase class InvalidValue(name: String, msg: String) extends ValidationError\n\n// Accumulate all errors instead of stopping at first\ncase class Validated[+E, +A](errors: List[E], value: Option[A]) {\n  def isValid: Boolean = errors.isEmpty && value.isDefined\n  def isInvalid: Boolean = !isValid\n}\n\nobject Validated {\n  def valid[A](a: A): Validated[Nothing, A] = Validated(List(), Some(a))\n  def invalid[E](e: E): Validated[E, Nothing] = Validated(List(e), None)\n\n  def combine[E, A](\n    results: List[Validated[E, A]]\n  ): Validated[E, List[A]] = {\n    val allErrors = results.flatMap(_.errors)\n    val allValues = results.flatMap(_.value)\n\n    if (allErrors.nonEmpty)\n      Validated(allErrors, None)\n    else\n      Validated(List(), Some(allValues))\n  }\n}\n\n// Example usage\ndef validateToken(json: String): Validated[ValidationError, Token] = {\n  val validations = List(\n    if (json.isEmpty) Validated.invalid(MissingField(\"json\")) else Validated.valid(json),\n    if (json.length > 1000) Validated.invalid(InvalidValue(\"json\", \"Too long\")) else Validated.valid(())\n  )\n  Validated.combine(validations).value.map(_ => Token(json, \"STRING\"))\n}\n```\n\n## 5. Custom Result Type\n\n### For Complex Error Handling\n```scala\nsealed trait Result[+A]\ncase class Success[A](value: A) extends Result[A]\ncase class Failure[A](errors: List[String]) extends Result[A]\n\nobject Result {\n  def apply[A](a: A): Result[A] = Success(a)\n\n  implicit class ResultOps[A](result: Result[A]) {\n    def map[B](f: A => B): Result[B] = result match {\n      case Success(a) => Success(f(a))\n      case Failure(errs) => Failure(errs)\n    }\n\n    def flatMap[B](f: A => Result[B]): Result[B] = result match {\n      case Success(a) => f(a)\n      case Failure(errs) => Failure(errs)\n    }\n\n    def getOrElse[B >: A](default: B): B = result match {\n      case Success(a) => a\n      case Failure(_) => default\n    }\n\n    def fold[B](onError: List[String] => B, onSuccess: A => B): B =\n      result match {\n        case Success(a) => onSuccess(a)\n        case Failure(errs) => onError(errs)\n      }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use Option for absence** - When a value may or may not exist\n2. **Use Either for errors with context** - When you need to communicate what went wrong\n3. **Use Try for exception safety** - When calling code that throws exceptions\n4. **Use sealed traits for error types** - Enables exhaustive pattern matching\n5. **Avoid null** - Always use Option\n6. **Chain operations** - Use map, flatMap, for-comprehensions\n7. **Match exhaustively** - Compiler helps ensure no cases missed\n8. **Provide meaningful error types** - Not just String messages\n9. **Consider accumulating errors** - When multiple validations needed\n10. **Avoid getOrElse in fallible contexts** - It hides errors\n\n## Comparison Table\n\n| Type | Use Case | Represents |\n|------|----------|-----------|\n| Option[T] | May or may not exist | Some(value) / None |\n| Either[E, T] | Error context needed | Left(error) / Right(value) |\n| Try[T] | Exception safety | Success(value) / Failure(ex) |\n| Boolean | Simple yes/no | true / false |\n| Null | AVOID | Nothing |\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1836,"cache_read_input_tokens":28709,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1836,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0a1caff8-68b0-47f1-8d8b-3a7ea67e28c4","uuid":"fe7910ff-b519-4e81-9e3e-f6f8e0185be1"}

Custom Result Type\n\n### For Complex Error Handling\n```scala\nsealed trait Result[+A]\ncase class Success[A](value: A) extends Result[A]\ncase class Failure[A](errors: List[String]) extends Result[A]\n\nobject Result {\n  def apply[A](a: A): Result[A] = Success(a)\n\n  implicit class ResultOps[A](result: Result[A]) {\n    def map[B](f: A => B): Result[B] = result match {\n      case Success(a) => Success(f(a))\n      case Failure(errs) => Failure(errs)\n    }\n\n    def flatMap[B](f: A => Result[B]): Result[B] = result match {\n      case Success(a) => f(a)\n      case Failure(errs) => Failure(errs)\n    }\n\n    def getOrElse[B >: A](default: B): B = result match {\n      case Success(a) => a\n      case Failure(_) => default\n    }\n\n    def fold[B](onError: List[String] => B, onSuccess: A => B): B =\n      result match {\n        case Success(a) => onSuccess(a)\n        case Failure(errs) => onError(errs)\n      }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use Option for absence** - When a value may or may not exist\n2. **Use Either for errors with context** - When you need to communicate what went wrong\n3. **Use Try for exception safety** - When calling code that throws exceptions\n4. **Use sealed traits for error types** - Enables exhaustive pattern matching\n5. **Avoid null** - Always use Option\n6. **Chain operations** - Use map, flatMap, for-comprehensions\n7. **Match exhaustively** - Compiler helps ensure no cases missed\n8. **Provide meaningful error types** - Not just String messages\n9. **Consider accumulating errors** - When multiple validations needed\n10. **Avoid getOrElse in fallible contexts** - It hides errors\n\n## Comparison Table\n\n| Type | Use Case | Represents |\n|------|----------|-----------|\n| Option[T] | May or may not exist | Some(value) / None |\n| Either[E, T] | Error context needed | Left(error) / Right(value) |\n| Try[T] | Exception safety | Success(value) / Failure(ex) |\n| Boolean | Simple yes/no | true / false |\n| Null | AVOID | Nothing |\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Naming Conventions\n\n### Package Names\n```scala\n// lowercase, no underscores, use reverse domain notation\npackage com.example.tokenizer\npackage org.apache.spark.sql\n\n// NOT: package Com.Example.Tokenizer (wrong - caps)\n// NOT: package com.example.token_izer (wrong - underscore)\n```\n\n### Class and Trait Names (PascalCase)\n```scala\nsealed trait TokenType\ncase class StringToken(value: String) extends TokenType\nclass TokenRegistry[T]\nobject TokenFactory\n\n// NOT: class string_token or class stringToken\n// NOT: trait tokenType or trait TOKEN_TYPE\n```\n\n### Method and Variable Names (camelCase)\n```scala\ndef tokenize(value: String): String = ???\ndef tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): List[(String, Int)] = ???\nval tokenValue = \"hello\"\nvar mutableCounter = 0\n\ndef _privateHelper(): String = ???  // Single leading underscore for private-ish\ndef __veryPrivate(): String = ???   // Double for extra emphasis (rarely used)\n\n// NOT: def Tokenize or def tokenize_with_positions\n// NOT: val TokenValue or val token_value\n```\n\n### Type Parameters (Single Upper Letters)\n```scala\nclass Container[T]\nclass Pair[A, B]\nclass Effect[F[_]]\n\n// More descriptive when needed\nclass Tokenizer[Value, TokenType]\n\n// Suffixes for variance hints\nclass Functor[F[_]]\nclass Consumer[-T]     // Contravariant - often suffixed with -T\nclass Producer[+T]     // Covariant - often suffixed with +T\n```\n\n### Constants (UPPER_CASE)\n```scala\nobject Config {\n  val DEFAULT_ENCODING = \"UTF-8\"\n  val MAX_TOKEN_LENGTH = 1000\n  val BUFFER_SIZE = 8192\n}\n\n// But in traits/classes, often use camelCase:\nclass TokenProcessor {\n  val maxRetries = 3\n  val defaultTimeout = 30000L\n}\n```\n\n### Boolean Methods\n```scala\ndef isEmpty: Boolean = ???\ndef isValid: Boolean = ???\ndef hasMetadata: Boolean = ???\ndef canProcess: Boolean = ???\n\n// NOT: def checkEmpty or def getIsEmpty\n```\n\n### Getter/Setter Naming\n```scala\n// Properties (no get/set prefix)\nclass Token {\n  def value: String = ???\n  def tokenType: TokenType = ???\n}\n\n// For vars (mutable)\nclass Buffer {\n  private var _items: List[String] = List()\n\n  def items: List[String] = _items\n  def items_=(newItems: List[String]): Unit = { _items = newItems }\n}\n```\n\n### Test Method Names\n```scala\ndef testTokenizationWithEmptyString(): Unit = ???\ndef testErrorHandlingWhenEncodingFails(): Unit = ???\n\n// Using test framework conventions (with ScalaTest):\n\"Tokenizer\" should {\n  \"tokenize strings correctly\" in { /* test */ }\n  \"handle null values gracefully\" in { /* test */ }\n}\n```\n\n## 2. Code Style Conventions\n\n### Brace Placement\n```scala\n// Opening brace on same line (Java/Scala style, not K&R)\ndef tokenize(value: String): String = {\n  value.toUpperCase\n}\n\n// NOT: def tokenize(value: String): String {\n```\n\n### Indentation\n```scala\n// 2 spaces (standard in Scala)\nclass Tokenizer {\n  def tokenize(value: String): String = {\n    if (value.isEmpty) {\n      \"\"\n    } else {\n      value.toUpperCase\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// NOT: 4 spaces (Java convention)\n```\n\n### Line Length\n```scala\n// Aim for 80-100 characters max\n// Break long lines at logical points\n\nval result = veryLongMethodName(firstParameter, secondParameter)\n  .chainedMethod(thirdParameter)\n  .anotherMethod()\n\ndef complexFunction(\n  parameter1: String,\n  parameter2: Int,\n  parameter3: Boolean\n): String = {\n  // implementation\n}\n```\n\n### Imports\n```scala\n// Group imports logically\nimport scala.collection.immutable.List\nimport scala.util.{Try, Success, Failure}\n\n// NOT: import scala._ (wildcard imports are okay for package objects)\n// Keep imports at top of file, organized\n```\n\n### Comments\n```scala\n// Use // for single-line comments\n\n/** Use /** */ for documentation (Scaladoc)\n  *\n  * @param value The input value\n  * @return The tokenized result\n  */\ndef tokenize(value: String): String = {\n  value.toUpperCase\n}\n\n// Multi-line explanation of complex logic\n// This algorithm uses a two-pass approach:\n// 1. First pass: identify boundaries\n// 2. Second pass: extract tokens\n```\n\n## 3. Idiomatic Patterns\n\n### Implicit Parameters\n```scala\n// Lowercase implicit names\nimplicit val stringOrdering: Ordering[String] = Ordering.String\n\n// Standard library names\ndef sort[T](items: List[T])(implicit ord: Ordering[T]): List[T] = ???\n\n// Context bounds are preferred when possible\ndef sort[T : Ordering](items: List[T]): List[T] = ???\n```\n\n### Extension Methods\n```scala\n// Implicit classes for extensions (Scala 2)\nimplicit class StringOps(s: String) {\n  def isTokenizable: Boolean = s.nonEmpty\n}\n\n// Or trait-based (Scala 2 & 3)\nimplicit class IntOps(i: Int) {\n  def toToken: String = i.toString\n}\n```\n\n### Object Naming\n```scala\n// Companion objects match class name exactly\nclass Token(val value: String)\nobject Token {\n  def empty: Token = Token(\"\")\n  def apply(value: String): Token = new Token(value)\n}\n\n// Module objects for groups of functions\nobject TokenUtils {\n  def normalize(s: String): String = s.trim.toLowerCase\n  def escape(s: String): String = s.replace(\"\\\"\", \"\\\\\\\"\")\n}\n\n// NOT: class Token ... object TokenHelper or object TokenOps\n```\n\n## 4. Deprecated Patterns (Avoid)\n\n```scala\n// WRONG: Scala-unfriendly Java style\nclass TokenContainer {\n  private String value;  // No, use val value: String\n\n  public String getValue() { return value; }  // No, use def value\n\n  public void setValue(String v) { value = v; }  // No, immutability preferred\n}\n\n// WRONG: Not idiomatic\nval myToken = Option(null).getOrElse(Token(\"default\"))  // Use Option[T]\n\nval result = if (x > 0) Some(x) else None  // Use Option(x) if x > 0\n```\n\n## 5. Scala-Specific Conventions\n\n### Underscore Usage\n```scala\n// Wildcard in pattern matching\ndef describe(x: Any): String = x match {\n  case _: String => \"String\"\n  case _: Int => \"Int\"\n  case _ => \"Unknown\"\n}\n\n// Placeholder in functions\nList(1, 2, 3).map(_ * 2)\n\n// Ignore values\nfor {\n  (key, _) <- map\n} println(key)\n\n// Bind/group patterns\ncase class Pair(a: Int, b: Int)\nval Pair(x, _) = pair\n```\n\n### Symbolic Operators\n```scala\n// Can define custom operators (use sparingly)\nimplicit class Vector(vals: List[Double]) {\n  def +(other: Vector): Vector = Vector(vals.zip(other.vals).map { case (a, b) => a + b })\n}\n\n// Standard operators (well-known)\nList(1, 2) ++ List(3, 4)  // Concatenation\nMap(\"a\" -> 1) ++ Map(\"b\" -> 2)  // Merge\n```\n\n## Comparison: Python vs Scala Naming\n\n| Purpose | Python | Scala |\n|---------|--------|-------|\n| Classes | `TokenType` | `TokenType` |\n| Methods | `tokenize_string` | `tokenizeString` |\n| Constants | `MAX_SIZE` | `MAX_SIZE` |\n| Private | `_private_method` | `private def privateMethod` |\n| Boolean | `is_valid()` | `isValid` |\n| Type Params | `T` | `T` |\n| Packages | `com.example` | `com.example` |\n\n## Best Practices Summary\n\n1. **Use PascalCase for classes/traits** - Immediately recognizable\n2. **Use camelCase for methods/values** - Fluent, readable\n3. **Use UPPER_CASE for constants** - Clear distinction\n4. **Prefix boolean methods with is/has** - Self-documenting\n5. **Use meaningful parameter names** - Avoid single letters except generics\n6. **2-space indentation** - Scala standard\n7. **80-100 char line limit** - Readable on any screen\n8. **Document public APIs** - Scaladoc comments\n9. **Avoid scala.Predef._ imports** - */\nsealed trait TokenType {\n  def value: String\n}\n\nobject TokenType {\n  case object STRING extends TokenType {\n    val value = \"string\"\n  }\n  case object NUMERIC extends TokenType {\n    val value = \"numeric\"\n  }\n  case object TEMPORAL extends TokenType {\n    val value = \"temporal\"\n  }\n  case object STRUCTURED extends TokenType {\n    val value = \"structured\"\n  }\n  case object BINARY extends TokenType {\n    val value = \"binary\"\n  }\n  case object NULL extends TokenType {\n    val value = \"null\"\n  }\n\n  def fromString(s: String): Option[TokenType] = s match {\n    case \"string\" => Some(STRING)\n    case \"numeric\" => Some(NUMERIC)\n    case \"temporal\" => Some(TEMPORAL)\n    case \"structured\" => Some(STRUCTURED)\n    case \"binary\" => Some(BINARY)\n    case \"null\" => Some(NULL)\n    case _ => None\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Core Token Data Structure\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Immutable token representation.\n  * Case class provides: equals, hashCode, toString, copy, pattern matching.\n  */\ncase class Token(\n    value: String,\n    tokenType: TokenType,\n    metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map()\n) {\n  /**\n    * Return new token with additional metadata.\n    * Immutable update using copy().\n    */\n  def withMetadata(newMetadata: (String, Any)*): Token = {\n    val merged = metadata ++ newMetadata.toMap\n    this.copy(metadata = merged)\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Type Classes for Duck Typing (replacing Python's Protocol)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Type class for objects that can be converted to tokens.\n  * Replaces Python's @runtime_checkable Protocol.\n  */\ntrait Tokenizable[T] {\n  def toToken(value: T): String\n}\n\nobject Tokenizable {\n  implicit val stringTokenizable: Tokenizable[String] = (s: String) => s\n  implicit val intTokenizable: Tokenizable[Int] = (i: Int) => i.toString\n  implicit val longTokenizable: Tokenizable[Long] = (l: Long) => l.toString\n  implicit val doubleTokenizable: Tokenizable[Double] = (d: Double) => d.toString\n  implicit val bigDecimalTokenizable: Tokenizable[BigDecimal] =\n    (b: BigDecimal) => b.toString\n}\n\n/**\n  * Type class for objects with length.\n  */\ntrait HasLength[T] {\n  def length(value: T): Int\n}\n\nobject HasLength {\n  implicit val stringHasLength: HasLength[String] = (s: String) => s.length\n  implicit val seqHasLength[T]: HasLength[Seq[T]] = (s: Seq[T]) => s.length\n  implicit val mapHasLength[K, V]: HasLength[Map[K, V]] = (m: Map[K, V]) => m.size\n}\n\n/**\n  * Type class for token processors (contravariant).\n  */\ntrait TokenProcessor[-T] {\n  def process(item: T): Unit\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Mutable Batch Container (contrast with immutable Token)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Mutable batch of tokens with state tracking.\n  * Demonstrates when mutability is necessary.\n  */\nclass MutableTokenBatch {\n  private var _tokens: List[Token] = List()\n  private var _processed: Boolean = false\n\n  def tokens: List[Token] = _tokens\n\n  def add(token: Token): Unit = {\n    if (_processed) {\n      throw new IllegalStateException(\"Batch already processed\")\n    }\n    _tokens = _tokens :+ token\n  }\n\n  def markProcessed(): Unit = {\n    _processed = true\n  }\n\n  def isProcessed: Boolean = _processed\n\n  def clear(): Unit = {\n    if (_processed) {\n      throw new IllegalStateException(\"Cannot clear processed batch\")\n    }\n    _tokens = List()\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Generic Container Classes with Variance Annotations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Covariant container - can return subtypes.\n  * The +T annotation allows List[Dog] to be used where List[Animal] is expected.\n  */\nclass TokenContainer[+T](items: Seq[T]) {\n  private val _items: Vector[T] = items.toVector\n\n  def getAll: Vector[T] = _items\n\n  def mapTokens[S](func: T => String): List[String] =\n    _items.map(func).toList\n\n  def size: Int = _items.size\n\n  def isEmpty: Boolean = _items.isEmpty\n\n  def apply(index: Int): Option[T] = {\n    if (index >= 0 && index < _items.length) Some(_items(index))\n    else None\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n  * Contravariant sink - can accept supertypes.\n  * The -T annotation allows a Consumer[Any] to be used where Consumer[String] is expected.\n  */\nclass TokenSink[-T] {\n  private var _received: List[Any] = List()\n\n  def receive(item: T): Unit = {\n    _received = _received :+ item\n  }\n\n  def drain(): List[Any] = {\n    val result = _received\n    _received = List()\n    result\n  }\n\n  def size: Int = _received.length\n}\n\n/**\n  * Invariant handler - exact type matching required.\n  * Can both read and write, so T appears in both input and output positions.\n  */\nclass BivariantHandler[T](initialValue: T) {\n  private var _value: T = initialValue\n\n  def get: T = _value\n\n  def set(value: T): Unit = {\n    _value = value\n  }\n\n  def transform(func: T => T): T = {\n    _value = func(_value)\n    _value\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Abstract Base Tokenizer with Generic Input Type\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Abstract base tokenizer with generic input type.\n  * Provides both eager and lazy tokenization.\n  */\nabstract class BaseTokenizer[T] {\n  def tokenize(value: T): Token\n\n  def tokenizeBatch(values: Iterable[T]): Iterator[Token] =\n    values.iterator.map(tokenize)\n\n  def tokenizeSeq(values: Seq[T]): List[Token] =\n    values.map(tokenize).toList\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Concrete Tokenizer Implementations\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for string and byte values.\n  *\n  * Type parameter bound: Either String or Array[Byte]\n  */\nclass StringTokenizer(\n    encoding: String = \"UTF-8\",\n    normalizer: Option[String => String] = None\n) extends BaseTokenizer[String] {\n\n  private val normalizeFunc = normalizer.getOrElse(identity[String])\n\n  override def tokenize(value: String): Token = {\n    val normalized = normalizeFunc(value)\n    Token(normalized, TokenType.STRING)\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeBytes(bytes: Array[Byte]): Token = {\n    val str = new String(bytes, encoding)\n    tokenize(str)\n  }\n}\n\nobject StringTokenizer {\n  def withLowercaseNormalizer(): StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = Some(_.toLowerCase))\n\n  def withTrimNormalizer(): StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = Some(_.trim))\n\n  def withComposedNormalizer(f: String => String, g: String => String): StringTokenizer =\n    new StringTokenizer(normalizer = Some(s => g(f(s))))\n}\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for numeric types with precision handling.\n  * Uses type class constraint for numeric operations.\n  */\nclass NumericTokenizer(\n    precision: Int = 6,\n    formatOptions: Map[String, Any] = Map()\n) extends BaseTokenizer[Number] {\n\n  override def tokenize(value: Number): Token = {\n    val strValue = value match {\n      case d: Double => f\"$d%.${precision}f\"\n      case f: Float => f\"$f%.${precision}f\"\n      case bd: BigDecimal => f\"$bd%.${precision}f\"\n      case i: Int => i.toString\n      case l: Long => l.toString\n      case _ => value.toString\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.NUMERIC, Map(\"original_type\" -> value.getClass.getSimpleName))\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeDouble(value: Double): Token = tokenize(value.asInstanceOf[Number])\n\n  def tokenizeInt(value: Int): Token = tokenize(value.asInstanceOf[Number])\n\n  def tokenizeBigDecimal(value: BigDecimal): Token = tokenize(value.asInstanceOf[Number])\n}\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for date/time types with customizable formats.\n  */\nclass TemporalTokenizer(format: Option[String] = None) extends BaseTokenizer[java.time.temporal.Temporal] {\n\n  private val ISO_FORMAT = \"yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss\"\n  private val DATE_FORMAT = \"yyyy-MM-dd\"\n\n  override def tokenize(value: java.time.temporal.Temporal): Token = {\n    val formatter = DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern(\n      format.getOrElse(\n        value match {\n          case _: LocalDateTime => ISO_FORMAT\n          case _: ZonedDateTime => ISO_FORMAT\n          case _: LocalDate => DATE_FORMAT\n          case _ => ISO_FORMAT\n        }\n      )\n    )\n\n    try {\n      val formatted = formatter.format(value)\n      Token(formatted, TokenType.TEMPORAL)\n    } catch {\n      case ex: Exception =>\n        Token(value.toString, TokenType.TEMPORAL, Map(\"error\" -> ex.getMessage))\n    }\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeLocalDate(date: LocalDate): Token = tokenize(date)\n\n  def tokenizeLocalDateTime(dateTime: LocalDateTime): Token = tokenize(dateTime)\n\n  def tokenizeZonedDateTime(zoned: ZonedDateTime): Token = tokenize(zoned)\n}\n\nobject TemporalTokenizer {\n  def withIsoFormat(): TemporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  def withFormat(fmt: String): TemporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer(Some(fmt))\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Union Type Handling with Pattern Matching (replaces Python overloads)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Universal tokenizer that handles multiple types via pattern matching.\n  * Scala's static typing handles dispatch more explicitly than Python.\n  */\nclass UniversalTokenizer {\n  private val stringTokenizer = new StringTokenizer()\n  private val numericTokenizer = new NumericTokenizer()\n  private val temporalTokenizer = new TemporalTokenizer()\n\n  /**\n    * Type-safe dispatch to appropriate tokenizer based on input type.\n    * Uses pattern matching instead of isinstance() checks.\n    */\n  def tokenize[T](value: T)(implicit tag: ClassTag[T]): Token = {\n    value match {\n      case null => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n      case s: String => stringTokenizer.tokenize(s)\n      case i: Int => numericTokenizer.tokenizeInt(i)\n      case l: Long => numericTokenizer.tokenize(l.asInstanceOf[Number])\n      case d: Double => numericTokenizer.tokenizeDouble(d)\n      case f: Float => numericTokenizer.tokenize(f.asInstanceOf[Number])\n      case bd: BigDecimal => numericTokenizer.tokenizeBigDecimal(bd)\n      case ld: LocalDate => temporalTokenizer.tokenizeLocalDate(ld)\n      case ldt: LocalDateTime => temporalTokenizer.tokenizeLocalDateTime(ldt)\n      case zdt: ZonedDateTime => temporalTokenizer.tokenizeZonedDateTime(zdt)\n      case bytes: Array[Byte] => stringTokenizer.tokenizeBytes(bytes)\n      case other =>\n        // Fallback: attempt string conversion\n        Token(other.toString, TokenType.STRING, Map(\"fallback\" -> true))\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize optional value - returns NULL token for None.\n    */\n  def tokenizeOption[T](value: Option[T])(implicit tag: ClassTag[T]): Token = {\n    value match {\n      case Some(v) => tokenize(v)\n      case None => Token(\"NULL\", TokenType.NULL)\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Tokenize with error handling via Try/Either.\n    */\n  def tokenizeSafe[T](value: T)(implicit tag: ClassTag[T]): Either[String, Token] = {\n    Try(tokenize(value)) match {\n      case Success(token) => Right(token)\n      case Failure(ex) => Left(ex.getMessage)\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Advanced: Registry with Complex Nested Generics\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Registry with complex nested generic types.\n  * Demonstrates mutable and immutable collection mixing.\n  */\nclass TokenRegistry[T] {\n  private var _registry: Map[String, TokenContainer[T]] = Map()\n  private var _handlers: List[T => Option[Token]] = List()\n\n  def register(key: String, container: TokenContainer[T]): Unit = {\n    _registry = _registry + (key -> container)\n  }\n\n  def addHandler(handler: T => Option[Token]): Unit = {\n    _handlers = _handlers :+ handler\n  }\n\n  def process(key: String): List[Option[Token]] = {\n    _registry.get(key) match {\n      case Some(container) =>\n        val results = collection.mutable.ListBuffer[Option[Token]]()\n        for (item <- container.getAll) {\n          var handled = false\n          for (handler <- _handlers if !handled) {\n            handler(item) match {\n              case Some(token) =>\n                results += Some(token)\n                handled = true\n              case None => ()\n            }\n          }\n          if (!handled) {\n            results += None\n          }\n        }\n        results.toList\n      case None => List()\n    }\n  }\n\n  def getRegistered(key: String): Option[TokenContainer[T]] = _registry.get(key)\n\n  def listKeys: List[String] = _registry.keys.toList\n\n  def clear(): Unit = {\n    _registry = Map()\n    _handlers = List()\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Functor and Monad Patterns (replacing Python's simulation)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Functor trait for composable transformations.\n  * Higher-kinded types are more natural in Scala than Python.\n  */\ntrait Functor[F[_]] {\n  def map[A, B](fa: F[A])(f: A => B): F[B]\n}\n\n/**\n  * Monad trait extending Functor with flat_map and pure.\n  */\ntrait Monad[F[_]] extends Functor[F] {\n  def pure[A](a: A): F[A]\n\n  def flatMap[A, B](fa: F[A])(f: A => F[B]): F[B]\n\n  // Derived implementation\n  def map[A, B](fa: F[A])(f: A => B): F[B] =\n    flatMap(fa)(a => pure(f(a)))\n}\n\n/**\n  * Concrete functor/monad for Token values.\n  * Demonstrates functional composition.\n  */\nclass TokenFunctor[T](val value: T) {\n  def map[S](func: T => S): TokenFunctor[S] =\n    new TokenFunctor(func(value))\n\n  def flatMap[S](func: T => TokenFunctor[S]): TokenFunctor[S] =\n    func(value)\n\n  def getOrElse[S >: T](default: S): S =\n    if (value == null) default else value.asInstanceOf[S]\n\n  def fold[S](f: T => S, default: S): S =\n    if (value == null) default else f(value)\n}\n\n/**\n  * Token monad with applicative operations.\n  */\nclass TokenMonad[T](val value: T) extends TokenFunctor[T](value) {\n  def ap[S](funcWrapped: TokenMonad[T => S]): TokenMonad[S] =\n    new TokenMonad(funcWrapped.value(value))\n}\n\nobject TokenMonad {\n  def pure[T](value: T): TokenMonad[T] = new TokenMonad(value)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// JSON Structure Tokenization with Recursive Types\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Sealed trait hierarchy for JSON values.\n  * Replaces Python's recursive JsonValue type alias.\n  */\nsealed trait JsonValue {\n  def toJson: String = this match {\n    case JsonNull => \"null\"\n    case JsonString(s) => s\"\"\"\"$s\"\"\"\"\n    case JsonNumber(n) => n.toString\n    case JsonBoolean(b) => b.toString\n    case JsonArray(values) => values.map(_.toJson).mkString(\"[\", \",\", \"]\")\n    case JsonObject(pairs) =>\n      pairs.map { case (k, v) => s\"\"\"\"$k\":${v.toJson}\"\"\" }.mkString(\"{\", \",\", \"}\")\n  }\n}\n\ncase object JsonNull extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonString(value: String) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonNumber(value: Double) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonBoolean(value: Boolean) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonArray(values: Vector[JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\ncase class JsonObject(values: Map[String, JsonValue]) extends JsonValue\n\n/**\n  * Tokenizer for JSON structures with recursive handling.\n  */\nclass JsonTokenizer(pretty: Boolean = false) {\n  def tokenize(value: JsonValue): Token = {\n    val jsonStr = if (pretty) {\n      formatJsonPretty(value, 0)\n    } else {\n      value.toJson\n    }\n    Token(jsonStr, TokenType.STRUCTURED, Map(\"json\" -> true))\n  }\n\n  private def formatJsonPretty(value: JsonValue, indent: Int): String = {\n    val spaces = \"  \" * indent\n    val nextSpaces = \"  \" * (indent + 1)\n    value match {\n      case JsonNull => \"null\"\n      case JsonString(s) => s\"\"\"\"$s\"\"\"\"\n      case JsonNumber(n) => n.toString\n      case JsonBoolean(b) => b.toString\n      case JsonArray(values) =>\n        if (values.isEmpty) \"[]\"\n        else {\n          val items = values.map(v => s\"${nextSpaces}${formatJsonPretty(v, indent + 1)}\")\n          \"[\\n\" + items.mkString(\",\\n\") + s\"\\n${spaces}]\"\n        }\n      case JsonObject(pairs) =>\n        if (pairs.isEmpty) \"{}\"\n        else {\n          val items = pairs.map {\n            case (k, v) => s\"\"\"${nextSpaces}\"$k\": ${formatJsonPretty(v, indent + 1)}\"\"\"\n          }\n          \"{\\n\" + items.mkString(\",\\n\") + s\"\\n${spaces}}\"\n        }\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Extract and tokenize a value at a JSON path.\n    * Supports dot-separated paths like \"user.name.first\"\n    */\n  def tokenizePath(value: JsonValue, path: String): Option[Token] = {\n    val parts = path.split(\"\\\\.\")\n    var current: JsonValue = value\n\n    for (part <- parts) {\n      current = current match {\n        case JsonObject(pairs) =>\n          pairs.get(part).getOrElse(return None)\n        case JsonArray(values) =>\n          if (part.matches(\"\\\\d+\")) {\n            val idx = part.toInt\n            if (idx >= 0 && idx < values.length) values(idx)\n            else return None\n          } else {\n            return None\n          }\n        case _ => return None\n      }\n    }\n\n    Some(tokenize(current))\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Whitespace Tokenizer - Basic Text Tokenization\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Basic tokenizer that splits text by whitespace with optional filtering.\n  * Demonstrates practical tokenization for text processing.\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n    lowercase: Boolean = false,\n    minLength: Int = 0,\n    maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n    stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) extends BaseTokenizer[String] {\n\n  private val punctuation: Set[Char] = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into a token string.\n    * Returns None if word should be filtered out.\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var processed = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n      processed = processed.dropWhile(punctuation.contains)\n        .reverse.dropWhile(punctuation.contains).reverse\n    }\n\n    if (lowercase) {\n      processed = processed.toLowerCase\n    }\n\n    if (processed.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else if (maxLength.exists(processed.length > _)) {\n      Some(processed.take(maxLength.get))\n    } else if (processed.isEmpty) {\n      None\n    } else {\n      Some(processed)\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Split text by whitespace and return list of tokens.\n    */\n  override def tokenize(text: String): Token = {\n    // This override adapts the abstract method signature\n    // WhitespaceTokenizer primarily works with tokenizeText\n    Token(text, TokenType.STRING)\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeText(text: String): List[Token] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\").toList\n    words.zipWithIndex.flatMap {\n      case (word, position) =>\n        processToken(word).map { processed =>\n          Token(\n            processed,\n            TokenType.STRING,\n            Map(\"position\" -> position, \"original\" -> word)\n          )\n        }\n    }\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): List[String] = {\n    tokenizeText(text).map(_.value)\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): List[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n    val result = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[(String, Int, Int)]()\n    var currentPos = 0\n\n    for (word <- words) {\n      val start = text.indexOf(word, currentPos)\n      val end = start + word.length\n\n      processToken(word).foreach { processed =>\n        result += ((processed, start, end))\n      }\n\n      currentPos = end\n    }\n\n    result.toList\n  }\n\n  def countTokens(text: String): Int = tokenizeText(text).length\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Builder Pattern with Fluent Interface\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Fluent builder for creating custom tokenizers.\n  * Demonstrates method chaining with type safety.\n  */\nclass TokenizerBuilder[T](\n    normalizers: List[String => String] = List(),\n    validators: List[T => Boolean] = List(),\n    metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map()\n) {\n\n  def withNormalizer(normalizer: String => String): TokenizerBuilder[T] =\n    new TokenizerBuilder(normalizers :+ normalizer, validators, metadata)\n\n  def withValidator(validator: T => Boolean): TokenizerBuilder[T] =\n    new TokenizerBuilder(normalizers, validators :+ validator, metadata)\n\n  def withMetadata(newMetadata: (String, Any)*): TokenizerBuilder[T] =\n    new TokenizerBuilder(normalizers, validators, metadata ++ newMetadata.toMap)\n\n  /**\n    * Build the final tokenizer function.\n    */\n  def build(): T => Token = { value =>\n    // Validate\n    for (validator <- validators) {\n      if (!validator(value)) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Convert to string\n    var strValue = value.toString\n\n    // Apply normalizers\n    for (normalizer <- normalizers) {\n      strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.STRING, metadata)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build with error handling via Either.\n    */\n  def buildSafe(): T => Either[String, Token] = { value =>\n    Try {\n      build()(value)\n    } match {\n      case Success(token) => Right(token)\n      case Failure(ex) => Left(ex.getMessage)\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Companion Object for TokenizerBuilder\n// ============================================================================\n\nobject TokenizerBuilder {\n  def apply[T](): TokenizerBuilder[T] = new TokenizerBuilder[T]()\n\n  def forStrings: TokenizerBuilder[String] = TokenizerBuilder[String]()\n    .withNormalizer(_.trim)\n\n  def forNumbers: TokenizerBuilder[Number] = TokenizerBuilder[Number]()\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Standalone Helper Functions (module-level functions)\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Module object containing utility functions.\n  * Replaces Python module-level functions.\n  */\nobject TokenizerUtils {\n\n  /**\n    * Universal tokenization function.\n    * Automatically dispatches based on type.\n    */\n  def tokenize[T](value: T)(implicit tag: ClassTag[T]): Token = {\n    val universal = new UniversalTokenizer()\n    universal.tokenize(value)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Batch tokenize with lazy evaluation.\n    */\n  def tokenizeBatch[T](values: Iterable[T])(tokenizer: T => Token): Iterator[Token] =\n    values.iterator.map(tokenizer)\n\n  /**\n    * Convert value to token using type class.\n    */\n  def toToken[T](value: T)(implicit ev: Tokenizable[T]): String =\n    ev.toToken(value)\n\n  /**\n    * Add metadata to an existing token.\n    */\n  def withMetadata(token: Token, metadata: (String, Any)*): Token =\n    token.withMetadata(metadata: _*)\n\n  /**\n    * Batch add metadata to multiple tokens.\n    */\n  def withMetadataBatch(\n      tokens: Seq[Token],\n      metadata: (String, Any)*\n  ): List[Token] =\n    tokens.map(_.withMetadata(metadata: _*)).toList\n\n  /**\n    * Filter tokens by type.\n    */\n  def filterByType(tokens: Seq[Token], tokenType: TokenType): List[Token] =\n    tokens.filter(_.tokenType == tokenType).toList\n\n  /**\n    * Group tokens by type.\n    */\n  def groupByType(tokens: Seq[Token]): Map[TokenType, List[Token]] =\n    tokens.groupBy(_.tokenType).map { case (ttype, tlist) => (ttype, tlist.toList) }\n\n  /**\n    * Merge multiple token batches into a single batch.\n    */\n  def mergeBatches(batches: Seq[MutableTokenBatch]): MutableTokenBatch = {\n    val merged = new MutableTokenBatch()\n    for (batch <- batches; token <- batch.tokens) {\n      merged.add(token)\n    }\n    merged\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Convert tokens to comma-separated string (simple serialization).\n    */\n  def serializeTokens(tokens: Seq[Token], delimiter: String = \",\"): String =\n    tokens.map(_.value).mkString(delimiter)\n\n  /**\n    * Find first token matching predicate.\n    */\n  def findToken(tokens: Seq[Token], predicate: Token => Boolean): Option[Token] =\n    tokens.find(predicate)\n\n  /**\n    * Count tokens of specific type.\n    */\n  def countByType(tokens: Seq[Token], tokenType: TokenType): Int =\n    tokens.count(_.tokenType == tokenType)\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Result Type for Explicit Error Handling\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Result type for explicit, composable error handling.\n  * More sophisticated than Option or Try.\n  */\nsealed trait TokenizationResult[+T]\n\ncase class TokenizationSuccess[T](value: T) extends TokenizationResult[T]\n\ncase class TokenizationFailure(errors: List[String]) extends TokenizationResult[Nothing]\n\nobject TokenizationResult {\n  def success[T](value: T): TokenizationResult[T] = TokenizationSuccess(value)\n\n  def failure(error: String): TokenizationResult[Nothing] =\n    TokenizationFailure(List(error))\n\n  def failure(errors: List[String]): TokenizationResult[Nothing] =\n    TokenizationFailure(errors)\n\n  implicit class TokenizationResultOps[T](result: TokenizationResult[T]) {\n    def map[S](f: T => S): TokenizationResult[S] = result match {\n      case TokenizationSuccess(value) => TokenizationSuccess(f(value))\n      case TokenizationFailure(errors) => TokenizationFailure(errors)\n    }\n\n    def flatMap[S](f: T => TokenizationResult[S]): TokenizationResult[S] =\n      result match {\n        case TokenizationSuccess(value) => f(value)\n        case TokenizationFailure(errors) => TokenizationFailure(errors)\n      }\n\n    def getOrElse[S >: T](default: S): S = result match {\n      case filtering.\n  * Demonstrates practical tokenization for text processing.\n  */\nclass WhitespaceTokenizer(\n    lowercase: Boolean = false,\n    minLength: Int = 0,\n    maxLength: Option[Int] = None,\n    stripPunctuation: Boolean = false\n) extends BaseTokenizer[String] {\n\n  private val punctuation: Set[Char] = Set(\".,!?;:'\\\"()[]{}\")\n\n  /**\n    * Process a single word into a token string.\n    * Returns None if word should be filtered out.\n    */\n  private def processToken(word: String): Option[String] = {\n    var processed = word\n\n    if (stripPunctuation) {\n (processed.length < minLength) {\n      None\n    } else if (maxLength.exists(processed.length > _)) {\n      Some(processed.take(maxLength.get))\n    } else if (processed.isEmpty) {\n      None\n the abstract method signature\n    // WhitespaceTokenizer primarily works with tokenizeText\n    Token(text, TokenType.STRING)\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeText(text: String): List[Token] = {\n    val words TokenType.STRING,\n            Map(\"position\" -> position, \"original\" -> word)\n          )\n        }\n    }\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeToStrings(text: String): List[String] = {\n    tokenizeText(text).map(_.value)\n  }\n\n  def tokenizeWithPositions(text: String): List[(String, Int, Int)] = {\n    val words = text.split(\"\\\\s+\")\n    val result = scala.collection.mutable.ListBuffer[(String, Int, Int)]()\n    var currentPos = 0\n\n    for (word <- words) {\n      val start = text.indexOf(word, currentPos)\n      val end = start + word.length\n\n      processToken(word).foreach { processed =>\n        result += ((processed, start, end))\n      }\n\n      currentPos = end\n    }\n\n    result.toList\n  }\n\n  def countTokens(text: String): Int = tokenizeText(text).length\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Builder Pattern with Fluent Interface\n// ============================================================================\n\n/**\n  * Fluent builder for creating custom tokenizers.\n  * Demonstrates method chaining with type safety.\n  */\nclass TokenizerBuilder[T](\n    normalizers: List[String => String] = List(),\n    validators: List[T => Boolean] = List(),\n    metadata: Map[String, Any] = Map()\n) {\n\n  def withNormalizer(normalizer: String => String): TokenizerBuilder[T] =\n    new TokenizerBuilder(normalizers :+ normalizer, validators, metadata)\n\n  def withValidator(validator: T => Boolean): TokenizerBuilder[T] =\n    new (!validator(value)) {\n        throw new IllegalArgumentException(s\"Validation failed for $value\")\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Convert to string\n    var strValue = value.toString\n\n    // Apply normalizers\n    for (normalizer <- normalizers) {\n      strValue = normalizer(strValue)\n    }\n\n    Token(strValue, TokenType.STRING, metadata)\n  }\n\n  /**\n    * Build with error handling via Either.\n    */\n  def buildSafe(): T => Either[String, Token] = { value =>\n    Try {\n      build()(value)\n    } match {\n      case Success(token) => Right(token)\n      case Failure(ex) =>
